## Portable custom Slicer VC143 initialization (2026-09-23)

| Boundary | Defect and correction | Regression guard |
|---|---|---|
| MSVC-built Slicer/VTK DLL -> older Windows Server VC runtime -> Client/Server payload | On Razi Windows Server 2022 the hash-matching native viewer failed before startup with `0xC0000142`. Isolated `LoadLibrary` identified `vtkCommon-9.5.dll` failing with Win32 1114 in both Session 0 and hidden console Session 1. The DLL loaded when the official compiler-matched VC143 14.44 runtime was supplied app-locally; `msvcp140.dll` alone was the minimal discriminating input. Assembly now pins and stages the complete 10-file VC143 x64 CRT set beside the inner viewer and the build parity gate rejects missing or changed app-local DLLs. No global redistributable or clinical service is changed. | `tests/code/builder/test_slicer_assembly_target.py::test_slicer_assembly_stages_exact_app_local_vc_runtime` and `test_slicer_runtime_current_source.py::test_portable_native_runtime_rejects_missing_or_wrong_app_local_crt` both failed before the fix (missing functions), then passed. Separate Razi candidate launcher `--version` and no-main-window synthetic Slicer/VTK probe exited 0; full installer/clinical acceptance remains pending. |

## Advanced MPR installed Slicer source parity (2026-09-22)

| Boundary | Defect and correction | Regression guard |
|---|---|---|
| Developer Run startup/presentation -> immutable Slicer cache -> both installer backends -> same-version module upgrade | The cached runtime carried a January startup script while Developer Run used the current source plus a separate presentation adapter. The frozen launcher could prefer the legacy script without its `presentation.py`/Qss companions. Packaging now overlays the current startup into each staged runtime, records both startup and complete Slicer Python/UI payload hashes, and rejects a cached native/UI runtime that differs from the assembled Developer Run. Installed launch prefers the complete packaged script. Bootstrap reinstalls an already selected Advanced MPR package when either content revision differs, even at the same product version; an invalid bundled hash cannot replace the prior runtime. | `tests/code/builder/test_slicer_runtime_current_source.py` and the same-version bootstrap tests in `tests/code/module_system/test_module_installation_packages.py` failed before correction for the reported boundaries and now cover script selection, native-cache rejection, presentation-only change, no repeated copy and corrupt-package preservation. Installed-machine UI acceptance remains a separate gate. |
| Frozen workstation warm-up -> external Slicer resident guard | The resident launcher used its frozen module `__file__` directory for Slicer's `--additional-module-path` and startup script, even though the authoritative Slicer files are deployed under the Advanced MPR runtime. Resolve both from that installed runtime for PyInstaller and Nuitka, retain source paths in Developer Run, and reject incomplete guard/presentation files before spawning Slicer. This prevents an unguarded stock window from being launched by automatic warm-up when packaged paths differ. | `tests/code/mpr/test_slicer_resident.py::test_frozen_warmup_loads_guard_and_presentation_from_installed_runtime` failed before the fix on the wrong module path and passed afterward; `::test_frozen_warmup_rejects_incomplete_runtime_before_process_creation` covers fail-closed startup. Fresh installed GUI validation is pending. |

## OPT-58 / OPT-60 inactive patient-tab thumbnail ownership (2026-09-19)

| Boundary | Defect and correction | Regression guard |
|---|---|---|
| Active patient tab -> Local inventory producer / prepared sidebar generation | Switching to another patient left the previous tab's Local inventory worker, prepared-image task and Qt card writer active. A cold 23-series owner scanned for 18.475 s while a subsequent indexed 66-series grouped sidebar rendered for 9.169 s, and the hidden grouped owner continued applying cards after a third patient opened. Activation now controls one visibility gate for both existing producers. Deactivation stops the Local GUI timer and pauses the worker at the next bounded series boundary; a currently running read may finish but cannot publish or advance to another inventory. The qasync sidebar may likewise finish one in-flight detached image preparation but cannot mutate hidden Qt widgets. Activation resumes the same generation, identity, row reservation and order. Close/supersession still use the existing cancellation/retirement paths. A bounded 50 ms owner re-check releases a paused coroutine after native destruction. Per-card INFO writes are sampled at card 1 and every tenth card; the terminal marker retains the exact total. `AIPACS_PATIENT_THUMBNAIL_VISIBILITY_GATE=0` restores legacy always-active presentation. No decoder, renderer, download protocol, cache format or DICOM grouping changed. | Two lifecycle guards failed before because no presentation lifecycle hook existed; a third reproduced linear per-card INFO emission and a fourth reproduced an indefinite inactive wait after native deletion. They now prove pause-without-retirement, no hidden disk advance/Qt mutation, exact ordered resume, rollback behavior, bounded progress logging and native-lifetime release. Local/sidebar suites: 66 passed. Adjacent inactive-result, signal-lifetime and lifecycle suites: 64 passed. Fresh-source rapid A -> B -> C single/multi-study GUI/KPI acceptance remains required. |

## OPT-58 / OPT-60 hidden Home thumbnail producer (2026-09-19)

| Boundary | Defect and correction | Regression guard |
|---|---|---|
| Home preview -> patient-tab activation -> shared image-preparation executor | Hiding Home stopped painting but did not stop its qasync image producer or progressive timer. In the live indexed 39-series case, Local inventory completed in tens of milliseconds while the hidden Home producer prepared all 39 images over 15.030 s; the patient sidebar consequently needed 18.822 s even though its maximum per-card GUI apply was 21.17 ms. `RightPanelWidget` now suspends its current generation on `hideEvent`, stops (without deleting) the owned timer, and wakes the same generation on `showEvent`. The worker admits no further file read while hidden; one already-running read may finish. Cards, action identity, render signature, order and prepared pixels are retained. No producer, cache, thread pool, viewer or download path was added. | `test_home_thumbnail_image_preparation.py::test_hidden_home_pauses_image_preparation_and_resumes_without_losing_cards` failed before for both immediate/progressive ownership (the hidden panel admitted every remaining read). It now proves one-read-at-most suspension, zero hidden publication, exact ordered resume and one card per series. Four adjacent Home render/manager suites pass 68 cases; ten broader Home/thumbnail/sidebar suites pass 163. Compilation and scoped diff checks pass. Fresh source GUI/KPI acceptance remains open. |

## OPT-35 / OPT-60 patient-open identity and tab admission (2026-09-19)

| Boundary | Defect and correction | Regression guard |
|---|---|---|
| Home row -> canonical patient-study open identity -> thumbnail/catalog lookup | A Local row could carry an empty primary Study UID while the owner-filtered resolver still found valid studies. The open path kept the empty value, so the thumbnail lookup interpreted the thumbnail root as a study directory, enumerated 2,635 unrelated study folders and returned no cards after 13.825 s. `finalize_open_study_identity` now promotes the first resolved UID, keeps it selected-first, rejects a selected UID outside the resolved owner set and fails closed when no study exists. The thumbnail filesystem helper also rejects an empty UID before path construction. No new resolver, index, cache or fallback was added. | `test_patient_study_set.py` and `test_patient_open_admission.py` failed before the contract existed and now cover blank-primary promotion, dedup/order, foreign/stale selection rejection, no-study rejection and the empty-root filesystem guard. |
| Patient-tab request -> capacity decision -> widget/pipeline construction | The fifth patient request constructed `PatientWidget` and started its asynchronous catalog/viewer work before `CustomTabManager` rejected the tab limit. The subsequent modal warning entered a nested Qt event loop while qasync tasks were runnable, producing re-entry errors and a 22.823 s failed open. `HomeTabService` now owns one reservation set and reserves capacity before any widget construction. Registration commits the reservation; every failure aborts it. The tab manager keeps its final capacity check as defense, and the warning is deferred until the active async step has returned. | `test_patient_open_admission.py` proves duplicate/capacity/concurrent-last-slot behavior, commit/abort cleanup and that the real patient-widget factory is never called at capacity. The shared opening-study set is service-owned rather than duplicated in the Home widget. |

## Catalog

| 2026-09-06 | Build documentation and release workflow | Multiple prominent runbooks recommended incompatible legacy PyInstaller, simple Nuitka, resumable, and six-edition paths, allowing humans or agents to write different outputs and mistake diagnostic artifacts for release candidates. Added root `BUILD.md` as the single entry point with measured three-lane policy, exact six-file contract, safety limits, size/content gates, and subordinate-document pointers. | `tests/code/builder/test_canonical_build_runbook.py` pins the authoritative route, all entry-document links, full artifact contract, and unsafe-shortcut prohibitions. The exact documented pre-build selection passes 114 tests. |
| 2026-09-10 | Canonical build output routing, recovery, and speed contract | The coordinator still exposed a `--final-repo` CLI redirect even though final delivery belongs only in two repository folders. Canonical V5 now sends one self-contained card per selected level: one targeted sagittal T2, one geometrically matched sagittal T1 when available, and at most four contiguous axial T2 frames confined to the measured slab. | 2026-05-29 | custom_tab_manager (title_bar_tabs_layout) | User-reported round-4 alignment regression: patient tabs rendered CENTRED in the title bar instead of left-pinned next to the AI-Pacs logo. Round-3 fix had removed the OUTER trailing addStretch from title_bar_layout, but the INNER title_bar_tabs_layout (inside the QScrollArea) also had no addStretch. With widgetResizable=True, the QScrollArea expanded the chip container to the viewport width; the QHBoxLayout has Fixed-size chips and no expanding child or stretch, so Qt distributed the leftover horizontal space evenly around the chips - they rendered centred. Root-cause fix: restore self.title_bar_tabs_layout.addStretch(1) at the END of the inner layout. QSpacerItem.sizeHint() is (0,0) so it does NOT contribute to the layout's preferred width - horizontal scrolling still works correctly when chip-strip natural width exceeds the viewport. _add_title_bar_tab_widget already uses count()-1 so new chips are inserted BEFORE the stretch, preserving left-to-right order. | tests/code/system/test_patient_tab_strip_width_guard.py::test_inner_chip_layout_has_trailing_addStretch, ::test_add_title_bar_tab_widget_inserts_before_stretch (2 new guards) |

| 2026-05-29 | right_panel_widget (THUMBNAIL_BOX_HEIGHT) | User-reported round-2 home-thumbnail-overlap regression: after bumping the card from 190x190 to 190x215 (so server-desc + image-count labels coexist), home-page cards STILL overlapped vertically. Bumping setVerticalSpacing(6 -> 14) didn't help. THUMBNAIL_BOX_HEIGHT was 190 — 25 px LESS than the real card. Container locked at 100 px short of what 4 cards need; QGridLayout compressed them and they visually overlapped. | 2026-05-29 | patient_table_widget (_on_patient_clicked) | User-reported double-click unreliability on the Home patient list: "double-click often behaves as if only a single click occurred — system reloads thumbnails instead of opening the patient. Users sometimes need to click multiple times rapidly before the patient opens." Root cause: `_on_patient_clicked` synchronously called `highlight_selected_row(row)` on every single click. That helper does clearSelection + selectRow + viewport().update() — which fires currentRowChanged TWICE and forces a synchronous repaint inside the itemClicked handler. On slower systems / under UI load that work pushed the second mouse press of the user's double-click past Qt's `doubleClickInterval` (~400 ms default Windows). Qt then classified the second press as a fresh single click → another `itemClicked` → another thumbnail reload. The explicit selection work was also REDUNDANT: setSelectionBehavior(SelectRows) + setSelectionMode(ExtendedSelection) already auto-clears other selections and selects the clicked row natively in mousePressEvent. Fix: skip `highlight_selected_row(row)` in the non-Ctrl branch of `_on_patient_clicked`; let Qt handle selection. Ctrl branch keeps `toggle_row_selection(row)` because Ctrl+click is supposed to opt out of clear-others. | tests/code/system/test_patient_click_double_click_guard.py (4 guards) |

| 2026-05-29 | right_panel_widget (setMinimumWidth + grid right margin) | User-reported round-3 dotted-border-clip regression. Rounds 1+2 bumped the right grid margin (14 → 22) thinking it would push the card away from the AlwaysOn 12 px vertical scrollbar — but with `setAlignment(AlignLeft)` on a fixed-width 190 px card, the card sits at x=left_margin regardless of how generous the right margin is. The grid right margin only matters when alignment is Center / Right or the child is Expanding. Real geometry: card_right_edge = 8 + 190 = 198; scrollbar_left_edge = panel_width − 12; visible_gap = panel_width − 210. With prior setMinimumWidth(200) the gap was −10 px at the floor — the dotted border literally clipped into the scrollbar. Fix: setMinimumWidth(200 → 232) guarantees ≥22 px gap at any width the splitter allows. Right margin also bumped 22 → 30 for visual breathing room at wider widths (not load-bearing). | tests/code/system/test_right_panel_min_width_guard.py (2 guards: min-width math + comment-points-at-geometry) |

| 2026-08-07 | modules/web_browser/prewarm (IMP-3) | User-reported: app froze on double-clicking patient 53516. Live pid 288604 — the OPT-22 idle gate approved the warm at 21:27:57, `kick()` then spent ~4.7 s in its background phase (QtWebEngine DLL import + 152 MB file warm), and `_on_construct` fired on the GUI thread at 21:28:01.8 WITHOUT re-checking input; `_finish_watch` had already removed the input filter, so the double-click landing in that window was invisible. Result: one 19.0 s MAIN_THREAD_STALL inside `_construct_warm_view`/`view.setUrl`, with the patient open processed only afterwards. Fix: `_finish_watch(warm=True)` keeps the input filter installed through `kick()`, and `_on_construct` re-checks input recency at construct time, deferring via the existing bounded IMP-1 poll/deadline. | 2026-08-08 | PacsClient/.../series_file_warm (new) + _hp_patient_open (WU-1) | User-reported: previously-downloaded patient 53417 took ~15.7 s for series 202 to reach the viewport. Root cause was NOT parsing — the switch-time header probe is already header-only with `specific_tags`; it was first-touch AV-scan + cold disk. Measured live: 444-file CT probed at 40.5 ms/file = 8.6 s; the same probe warm is 0.88 ms/file (~0.4 s), and the adaptive thread pool caps at ~2.3x (bench: seq 23.1 / t8 9.8 / t24 9.8 ms/file), so more threads are not the lever — warmth is. Fix: at patient open a budgeted, read-only daemon thread pre-reads the head of every on-disk instance file, paying the AV verdict + OS cache fill off the critical path. The switch-time scan and its full per-file verification are unchanged. Kill switch `AIPACS_SERIES_FILE_WARM=0`. Known limitation: a no-op for server studies opened before download (logs `files=0`). | `tests/code/viewer/test_series_file_warm.py` (12 guards) |

| 2026-08-16 | modules/web_browser/prewarm (IMP-4) | **72.1 second frozen clinical workstation.** Live pid 218252: `11:05:47 idle 12692ms >= 5000ms after first interaction -> warming now`, `11:06:04 file warm read 152.1 MB in 4358 ms (off-thread)`, `11:07:16 Chromium engine warmed (construct+setUrl 72122 ms on GUI thread)`. Every scheduling guard (OPT-22 idle gate, IMP-1 modal veto, IMP-3 recency veto) behaved CORRECTLY — the user really had been idle 12.7 s. The lesson from four live incidents (~17 s 2026-07-23, 39.7 s 2026-08-05, 19 s 2026-08-07, 72 s here) is that WHEN the construct runs was never the problem: its COST is unbounded and cannot be capped, because Qt requires the construction on the GUI thread and the call is atomic. Fix: `should_prewarm()` default flipped from adaptive-on to OPT-IN — the Chromium boot is now paid when the user actually opens the browser, an explicit action where a wait is expected. Opt in with `AIPACS_BROWSER_PREWARM=1` (only a literal `"1"`; the adaptive used-marker still gates on top). | `tests/code/system/test_browser_prewarm_idle_gate.py::test_prewarm_is_opt_in_by_default`, `::test_marker_gate_applies_on_top_of_the_opt_in`, `::test_only_an_explicit_1_enables_the_prewarm` (7 params) |

| 2026-08-16 | modules/viewer/fast/disk_pixel_cache | ~4 s of a contiguous 9.1 s frozen UI on the first series switch. `DiskPixelCache.initialize()` (an `iterdir` per study dir + a `stat` per cache file) ran synchronously on whichever thread first touched the module singleton — in practice the GUI thread. Measured live: 44 study dirs / 1,593 `.apc` files / ~1.97 GB; stall stack `disk_pixel_cache.initialize -> Path.iterdir / Path.stat`; and it grows with the cache. Fix: `initialize()` stays SYNCHRONOUS for every direct caller (no existing user or test changes behaviour); only `get_disk_pixel_cache()` passes `background=True` and indexes on a daemon thread. Safe because an unindexed lookup is simply a cache MISS. The merge skips keys a concurrent `put()` already registered (no double-counted bytes) and re-sorts by access time (the index's ORDER is the LRU order — `_evict_if_needed` pops the front — so appending old scanned entries after fresh ones would make the newest slices the first evicted). Measured GUI cost 90 ms -> 0.33 ms warm (the live 4 s was the cold/AV-scanned first touch). Kill switch `AIPACS_PIXEL_CACHE_ASYNC_INIT=0`. | `tests/code/viewer/test_disk_pixel_cache_async_init.py` (10 guards incl. a threaded writer-vs-scan race) |

| 2026-08-16 | PacsClient/.../_hp_search (VS-2 import warm) | The rest of the 9.1 s series-open freeze was ONE-TIME MODULE IMPORTS caught mid-import by the stall sampler: `cv2/__init__.py:181 <module> -> :153 bootstrap -> :88 import_module` (~2 s, verified by matching line numbers against the installed file) and `auto_window_level_from_array -> np.percentile -> np.unique -> numpy/__init__.py:737 __getattr__` (~1 s) — the numpy frame being `__getattr__` is the giveaway that this is import cost, not arithmetic. Fix: a `viewer-import-warm` daemon thread added to the EXISTING first-search warm hook (which already warms the download manager and the viewer linecache) imports `cv2`, forces the numpy percentile/unique lazy path on a 3-element array, and imports `opencv_filter_pipeline`. Pure imports, no Qt object created. Measured with the PacsClient package already loaded (as in the running app): 187 ms warm vs ~3 s cold. Kill switch `AIPACS_VIEWER_IMPORT_WARM=0`. | `tests/code/viewer/test_viewer_import_warm.py` (8 guards, incl. one that fails if the warm ever touches a Qt object and one that fails if the windowing path stops using `np.percentile`) |

| 2026-08-16 | modules/web_browser/prewarm (IMP-5) | Warm-up optimisation after IMP-4. Phase-timing the boot showed the throwaway `QWebEngineView` was never the expensive part: `import QtWebEngineCore` 46 ms, **`QWebEngineProfile.defaultProfile()` 918 ms (the global init, GUI-thread only)**, `QWebEngineView()` **0 ms**, `setUrl()` + `loadFinished` 554 ms. Fix (a): `_construct_warm_view()` now just touches `defaultProfile()` — identical benefit for ~24 % less GUI-thread block (705 ms measured on the real path vs 889-991 ms), no page load, and no render process held 60 s to be discarded; verified against the REAL browser's NAMED profile (`QWebEngineProfile("aipacs-web-browser")`): no warm 888/803 ms -> warmed 140/132 ms. Fix (b): the off-thread file warm covered only 152.1 MB of a 358 MB payload; the 277 MB missed was dominated by `Qt6WebEngineCore.dll` (202.3 MB), which `kick()`'s import memory-maps but whose pages fault in lazily ON the GUI thread — now pre-read by name (72 files / 394.8 MB). Chromium flags are NOT a lever (measured `--disable-gpu` 661, `--no-sandbox` 665, both 635, `--in-process-gpu` 696 ms — all noise). Live confirmation 13:27: `defaultProfile 208 ms on GUI thread`. | `tests/code/web_browser/test_prewarm_idle_gate.py::test_warm_uses_default_profile_not_a_throwaway_view`, `::test_engine_dlls_are_included_in_the_off_thread_file_warm`, `::test_dll_warm_is_name_scoped_not_a_blanket_dll_sweep`, `::test_file_warm_still_budget_capped` |

| 2026-08-16 | modules/viewer/fast/disk_pixel_cache + PacsClient/.../mainwindow_ui | The L2 disk pixel cache was WIPED ON EVERY SHUTDOWN, so it had never once served a cross-session hit. `_shutdown_caches()` ended with `get_disk_pixel_cache().clear()` (`mainwindow_ui.py:1521`), and `clear()` is a `shutil.rmtree` of the cache root — not an index reset. Measured across `viewer_diagnostics.log*`: **18 "Disk pixel cache cleared" between 2026-08-08 and 2026-08-16, and both "indexed" lines report `0 entries`.** The module's own docstring calls it the "L2 persistent cache … eliminates the need to re-decode slices when reopening a previously-viewed series" and ships a 2 GB cap, LRU eviction and a startup index scan to support that; none of it could ever pay off. (The same-day async-init fix made that startup scan free — of an empty directory.) In-suite benchmark on real data: disk cache read 0.00 ms vs pydicom decode 3.41 ms = **341x**. Fix: new `DiskPixelCache.clear_on_exit()` — keeps the cache by default, wipes only when the site opts in. Deliberately a SEPARATE method, not a flag inside `clear()`, so an explicit user-initiated clear always clears; the in-memory caches in the same shutdown step are still cleared unconditionally. Bounded by the existing 2 GB cap + LRU, and `_scan_index` re-sorts by mtime so the LRU order survives the restart (without it, the slice viewed last before closing would be first evicted next session). `user_data/` is gitignored, so persisted pixels cannot reach VCS. Trade-off accepted by the owner: up to 2 GB of decoded patient pixels now persist on disk — same data already stored unencrypted as source DICOM, but for longer. Kill switch `AIPACS_PIXEL_CACHE_CLEAR_ON_EXIT=1` restores the wipe. Report: `docs/reports/PIXEL_CACHE_PERSISTENCE_2026-08-16.md`. | `tests/code/viewer/test_disk_pixel_cache_persistence.py` (20 guards; verified 13 FAIL on the pre-fix codebase, incl. an AST pin on the call site that a comment naming `.clear()` cannot fool) |

| 2026-08-16 | modules/network/ino_assignment_server_state + ino_assignment_refresh | **10.79 s frozen clinical UI** (live pid 239928, 17:19:41; 13.7 s of blocked GUI time in one minute). `refresh_assignments._one()` called `set_state` PER RECEPTION, and each call rewrites the WHOLE snapshot under the store's global `_LOCK` (`_load` -> mutate one key -> `json.dump` + `flush` + `fsync` + `os.replace`). `get_state` — called PER PATIENT-LIST ROW on the GUI thread — takes that same `_LOCK`, so refresh and repaint were serialised one full file write at a time. The sampler caught the GUI thread parked on `with _LOCK:` in `get_state` in **10 of 12 samples**. Measured on the live snapshot (356,972 bytes / 1,360 receptions, two real-time AV engines): one write **141 ms median / 210 ms p90 with fsync, 13 ms without**; `json.dumps` alone 3.4 ms; `get_state`'s cached read **0.023 ms** — the read was never the problem, the lock was, and fsync was 90 % of what it was held for. Arithmetic matches: 10793 / ~118 = 91 writes = one worklist refresh. WORSE OVER TIME: the write is O(all receptions) for ONE reception's update, so a full 1,360-row refresh was 192 s of lock-held time. Fix (a): new `set_many()` — build all entries first, then ONE lock acquisition, ONE `_load`, ONE `_save`; the refresh persists after the fan-out instead of per row. Fix (b): the fsync is opt-in — safe HERE because this file is a re-fetchable display cache (`os.replace` is still atomic, `_load` already swallows a parse error, the server is the source of truth). Both write paths build the record through one shared `_entry()` so they cannot drift. `_merge_and_save` loads INSIDE the same acquisition as the save, or a concurrent Assign-dialog `set_state` would be silently rolled back. Deliberately unchanged: `get_state` still takes the lock (that was the 2026-07-31 WinError-5 fix), `on_row` still fires per row, a failed fetch still leaves the snapshot alone, an interrupted refresh still persists what it fetched. **Result: a 91-reception refresh went from 12.8 s to 13 ms of lock-held time — 990x.** Kill switch `AIPACS_INO_STATE_FSYNC=1`. Report: `docs/reports/ASSIGNMENT_SNAPSHOT_BATCH_WRITE_2026-08-16.md`. | `tests/code/network/test_ino_state_batch_write.py` (28 guards; verified 11 FAIL on the pre-fix codebase — the 17 that still pass are the invariants that must hold in both modes) |

| 2026-08-16 | PacsClient/.../_pw_sync (`_rl_all_pairs_enabled`) | User-reported on imported study I10027433471 (SENAC--FRICHETEAU, cervical MR): a 2-up layout — SAG T1 TSE left, AX T2 TSE CHARNIERE right and ACTIVE — drew a yellow reference line in BOTH viewports, including the one being scrolled. NOT a defect: `_manage_reference_line_all_pairs` does exactly what it documents ("draw, on EVERY viewport, one reference line per OTHER viewport") and already refuses to self-reference (`if srec is trec: continue`), so 2 viewports x 1 line each = the observed 2. The imported-study angle was a red herring — the geometry was correct. It is still wrong for READING: the reader's convention is that the active series is the SOURCE and stays clean, with the line on the series being cross-referenced; a line across the cord on the T2 axial you are actively scrolling is noise over the anatomy. Product call, so it was taken to the owner before any edit; confirmed "no line on the active viewport". Fix: `_rl_all_pairs_enabled()` default flipped to OFF, which routes `manage_reference_line()` to the legacy single-source path that ALREADY implements this — and, critically, CLEARS the source overlay (`clear_overlay_lines` / `rl_hide_actor_if_any`) rather than merely skipping it, so a line drawn while a viewport was inactive vanishes the moment it becomes active. Verified the legacy path handles `IS_QT_BRIDGE` everywhere before trusting it (a pre-Qt-bridge path would have silently killed reference lines in FAST mode). Flag vocabulary inverted (enables on `1/true/yes/on`) so a site that had explicitly set `0` is unaffected and a garbled value falls to the safe side. Nothing deleted: `_manage_reference_line_all_pairs` and its tests are untouched; `AIPACS_REFERENCE_LINES_ALL_PAIRS=1` restores bidirectional. Two existing tests pinned the OLD default and were re-pointed, not deleted. Not a mystery about intent: the Advanced (VTK) backend has opened `QInputDialog.getText(None, "Enter Text", "Text:")` since forever in `text_interactorstyle.on_left_button_press` — the DEFAULT backend was the one that had never implemented it. Same title/label as the VTK backend so both viewers ask the same question. Fix (c), found while confirming the VTK path: `ok` was collected from `getText` and then IGNORED, so Cancel still added an empty `vtkVectorText` actor plus a store entry — an invisible label the user could neither see nor delete; added a bail-out before `create_text_actor`. Deliberately unchanged: a bare `ToolController` still places `"Text"` (every existing tool test depends on it); single-line input, because `_render_text` is a single `painter.drawText` and multi-line would silently swallow newlines; no new env flag, since one would only let a site opt back into the reported bug; the `builder/plugin package/…/payload/` copies, whose git history shows they are refreshed only at release commits. | 2026-08-18 | modules/ai_imaging/.../widgets (report_editor_dialog + new report_capture_images, report_image_picker_dialog) | FEATURE, owner-requested: physicians capture key images in the Patient tab and want them in the report, under the paragraph describing the finding. The Medical Report Editor could insert a link, a table and a horizontal line — verified against HEAD, `_insert_*` was exactly `{bullet_list, horizontal_line, link, number_list, table}` — and there was no path from a capture to the report, even though the captures were already on disk (`ATTACHMENT_PATH/<study_uid>/`) and already listed by the viewer's "View Captured Images" dropdown. Added: an **Insert Captured Image** button plus **Smaller / Larger / Fit-to-width**, all in the format toolbar's Insert group; a modal thumbnail gallery scoped to the current study (double-click inserts, newest first, capture-time captions, Refresh, empty state); and a downscale+embed encoder. The picker's listing mirrors `ImageAttachmentsPanel._iter_files` byte for byte (same extension tuple, mtime-descending, same local/server duplicate collapsing) with an AST guard that fails if the two diverge — otherwise the physician sees one set of images in the viewer and a different set in the report. Thumbnails decode 4 per event-loop tick via `QImageReader.setScaledSize`: a study can hold 30+ full-res PNGs and decoding them in the constructor blocks the GUI thread for seconds on this machine. EMBEDDING IS THE DESIGN DECISION: the report uploads to INO as ONE JSON field, so a `file:///` src would be a broken image for every reader but the author; the bytes must travel. Raw captures are 2-6 MB PNG and base64 adds ~33 %, so images are capped at 1000 px and re-encoded JPEG q88 — measured 224.8 KB PNG -> 78.9 KB JPEG -> 105.2 KB of data URI. `AIPACS_REPORT_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES` (1.5 MB) is a hard ceiling; on overshoot the encoder walks a quality/width ladder and REFUSES rather than handing the upload something it will reject. Resize writes width/height on the `QTextImageFormat`, which Qt emits as `<img width= height=>` attributes the server normaliser passes through — a CSS class would be stripped. MEASURED AND ACTED ON: the design assumed Qt's `loadResource` cannot decode `data:` URIs, but Qt 6.10.2 resolves them natively, so `install_data_uri_image_support` now PROBES once per process and no-ops when Qt already handles it — the compatibility subclass survives for an older/regressed Qt without putting a live document swap on the shipped path. BUG CAUGHT IN DEVELOPMENT, and the reason the resize guards are behavioural: the first probe used a REVERSED cursor selection, and `charFormat()` returns the character before `position()`, so it read the far side of the range — every resize button was wired, every handler ran, the width never changed, and every AST guard still passed. Deliberately not done: no new dependency (QImage, not QPixmap, so the helper needs no QApplication); no PDF export (none exists for reports; printing goes through `QTextEdit.print_` and renders the embedded image as shown); no drag-resize handles (QTextEdit has no native support — owner chose +/- buttons). SECOND BUG, REPORTED FROM THE FLOOR after the first cut shipped: the button said "This report is not linked to a study" for reception 54800 — a patient with the CT open behind the dialog and a capture already on disk. A report opened from the Reception Data tab is handed `reception_data_tab.current_data`, which is a RECEPTION record (`receptionId`, `nationalCode`, a `patient` sub-dict) with NO `studyUID` — while captures are keyed by study UID, so the check could only ever fail there. It survived review because the first cut reused the SAME key precedence as `_start_previous_exams_lookup` and a guard pinned the two together: they agreed, and were both empty. Fix: `resolve_study_uids()` falls back to the local DICOM DB, and the trap worth remembering is the join — `studies.patient_fk` is a FOREIGN KEY to `patients.patient_pk`, NOT the DICOM PatientID, so `WHERE patient_fk='54800'` returns zero rows silently (measured); it must go `JOIN patients p ON p.patient_pk = s.patient_fk WHERE p.patient_id = ?`. Identifiers are tried IN ORDER and the FIRST match wins — unioning every match was rejected because a national code colliding with another patient's `patient_id` would mix a DIFFERENT patient's key images into the report, a clinical error rather than a cosmetic one. The picker now accepts several study UIDs and labels thumbnails by study when there is more than one. Verified live: reception 54800 -> `1.2.840.1.99.1.47.1.1787052434018.87326` -> the capture taken that afternoon. Kill switch `AIPACS_REPORT_IMAGE_DB_LOOKUP=0`. Report: `docs/reports/REPORT_IMAGE_INSERT_2026-08-18.md`. | `tests/code/reporting/test_report_image_insert.py` (68 guards; `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_report_image_guard_fails_prefix.py` confirms 9 FAIL at HEAD via `git show`. Load-bearing: `test_the_upload_normaliser_keeps_the_image` — the normaliser already strips `<style>`/`<script>`/chrome and is one `_DIR_BLOCK_TAGS` entry from stripping `<img>`, which would lose the image for the referring doctor while the author's copy still shows it; `test_making_an_image_smaller_actually_changes_its_width` and `test_an_image_is_found_when_the_cursor_sits_just_after_it` — the behavioural pins the AST guards could not have caught; `test_a_document_can_render_a_data_uri` — pins BEHAVIOUR not mechanism, so it passes with or without the subclass; `test_the_extension_set_matches_the_viewer_dropdown`; `test_an_impossible_byte_ceiling_refuses_rather_than_overshooting`) |

| 2026-08-19 | modules/mpr/zeta_mpr/mpr_viewer (_mpr_layout, _mpr_series, _mpr_views + new _mpr_lifecycle) + PacsClient/.../patient_tab/utils/utils + .../patient_widget_core/_pw_lifecycle | Owner asked, after MPR-ing several series on patient 54921, whether "the cash free successfully". The MPR teardown is excellent — `_MprLayoutMixin.cleanup()` releases GPU resources, `Finalize()`s the render windows, drops the flipped host volume and breaks the interactor-style cycles — but it was reachable from **exactly one place**, the toolbar's MPR toggle (`toolbar_manager._restore_selected_viewer`). Log ledger of "STANDARD MPR VIEWER INITIALIZATION STARTED" vs "mpr_widget.cleanup() completed" across every logged process: **14 opened, 6 freed**; and inside one process, the smoking gun — `12:57:02 MPR opened (never toggled off) -> 15:35:03 close_patient -> 17:45:19 toggle scan finds active_mpr_widget: None, with no cleanup() in between`. That activation's volume, 4 render windows and GPU texture were orphaned; the process measured **1,931 MB RSS**. THE NON-OBVIOUS PART, and the reason the guards are shaped as they are: **a `closeEvent` hook alone does NOT fix this** — Qt does not call `closeEvent` when a parent is destroyed or when a widget is re-parented away, which is precisely how all three leaking paths worked (patient-tab close, `delete_widgets_in_layout` doing only `setParent(None)`, app exit). Fix (a): new `_mpr_lifecycle.release_mpr_children()`, duck-typed on the teardown contract (`cleanup` + `_mpr_closed`) rather than the class so it imports into `patient_tab.utils` without a cycle; never raises; skips already-closed viewers so the release count stays honest; deliberately NOT a `findChildren` sweep since hosts always publish the viewer under one of four known attributes. Fix (b): both owners call it **BEFORE** dropping the widget — order is the whole point, because after `setParent(None)` the GL context is gone and `ReleaseGraphicsResources()` can no longer free the VRAM; two guards assert the ORDERING, not just the presence of the call. Fix (c): `closeEvent` on the layout mixin for the explicit-close case (`cleanup()` is idempotent, so a close after a toggle is a no-op). Fix (d): `_reload_with_series` never re-pointed the 3D `vtkGPUVolumeRayCastMapper`, so every in-MPR series switch pinned one full host volume (~30-95 MB) plus its GPU texture — and rendered the OLD series in the 3D pane next to three panes showing the new one, a reading hazard as much as a memory one. Fix (e): `[MPR-MEM]` probes at open and at both ends of teardown with a `freed_mb` delta, unthrottled on purpose (throttling is exactly what would ruin an open/close pair) — answering this question took a four-log reconstruction because the MPR timings are in `viewer_diagnostics.log` while the close path logs to `app.log` and NEITHER carried a memory number. ALSO FIXED: `test_mpr_defer_3d_view.py::test_cleanup_clears_pending_flag` failed on the first regression run — its assertion was still true, but it bounds its search at a FIXED 3,000 characters from `def cleanup(` and the new probe pushed `_full_teardown` out of range, raising ValueError. That window had already been widened 600 -> 3000 on 2026-08-01 and a sibling test in the same file was re-bounded at the next `def` on 2026-08-18 for the identical reason; it is now bounded at the next `def` too, assertion untouched. The probe was also moved BELOW the two stop-accepting flags in `cleanup()` — those are safety-critical and diagnostics must never delay them. Deliberately not done: `cleanup()` itself is unchanged (this is entirely about REACHING it); no `gc.collect()` (the `crosshair_styles` -> style -> viewer cycle survives cleanup, but the large buffers are freed explicitly and this codebase avoids stop-the-world collections on the GUI thread); no app-exit hook (the driver reclaims VRAM at process death); `modules/mpr/orthogonal/` left alone (it has `cleanup`/`closeEvent` but is dead code — its only entry point has zero callers). Kill switches `AIPACS_MPR_RELEASE_ON_DESTROY=0`, `AIPACS_MPR_MEM_PROBE=0`. Report: `docs/reports/MPR_LIFECYCLE_RELEASE_2026-08-19.md`. | `tests/code/mpr/test_mpr_lifecycle_release.py` (28 guards; `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_mpr_lifecycle_guard_fails_prefix.py` confirms 12 FAIL at HEAD via `git show`. Load-bearing: `test_layout_teardown_releases_before_orphaning` and `test_patient_close_releases_the_mpr_child` — they pin the two paths that ACTUALLY leaked including the ordering, so a "simplification" down to just the closeEvent comes back green on the closeEvent tests and is caught by these; `test_series_switch_actually_repoints_the_3d_mapper` — behavioural on real VTK objects, asserting the mapper's input dimensions really changed; `test_an_already_closed_viewer_is_not_torn_down_twice` and `test_a_deleted_cpp_object_never_raises` — teardown races are normal on this path) |

| 2026-08-21 | PacsClient/pacs/workstation_ui/home_ui (patient_table_widget + home_search_service) | Owner-reported: "long freeze / lag during import… the more previously downloaded local studies there are, the worse the freeze becomes", up to full unresponsiveness. MEASURED in `user_data/logs/app.log` for the 13:49:30 import of study `…20260707080007251.1`: the main-thread probe logged **137 `[MAIN_THREAD_STALL_TRACE]` samples** in the window, and between 13:50:03 and 13:50:16 `gap_ms` climbed monotonically 2,913 -> 3,920 -> … -> **13,031 ms**, i.e. ONE uninterrupted 13-second block of the GUI thread. Ranked by innermost frame: **73 samples in `pathlib stat` (Σ 168 s) and 31 in `pathlib iterdir` (Σ 81 s) — 104 of 137 (76 %) inside a blocking filesystem call**, every one reached through `_progressive_render_next -> _progressive_background_step -> render_one -> _resolve_renderable_study_path -> Path.exists()/has_subfolders()`. `_progressive_render_next` appears in 122 of the 137 traces. THE NON-OBVIOUS PART: this work had ALREADY been moved off the GUI thread by OPT-50 (2026-08-03) — head 60 rows resolved before first paint, tail on a worker future — but `render_one` kept an **inline fallback** for "a row the worker has not reached yet", and the source comment asserted "the worker comfortably outruns the 40-rows-per-50 ms streamer… the race is harmless by construction". Measured warm, that is true: **0.16–0.22 ms/row = 4,500–6,000 rows/s vs the streamer's 800 rows/s**. It is false during an import, when the worker and the streamer share a disk that the importer is writing and on-access AV is re-scanning: the 13 s batch rendered 40 rows, i.e. **~325 ms per row**, three orders of magnitude slower. Once the streamer overtakes the worker EVERY remaining row pays that on the GUI thread — with 2,333 studies in the local DB that is thousands of rows, which is exactly why the freeze scales with library size. Fix (a): `load_progressive(…, ready=)` takes a "can this row be rendered without touching the disk?" predicate (`_row_paths_ready`, one dict membership test); a not-ready row **ends the batch** instead of being resolved inline, and the idle timer re-arms, so the stream FOLLOWS the worker rather than racing it. Fix (b): a per-batch wall-clock budget (`_PROGRESSIVE_BUDGET_MS = 30`) — even fully-resolved rows build cell widgets and 40 of those can outlast a frame. Fix (c): forward-progress escape hatch — continuous deferral for `_PROGRESSIVE_DEFER_FORCE_MS` (4 s) force-renders exactly ONE row per batch, so a dead resolver future can never stall the list permanently. Fix (d): a zero-render batch no longer rebuilds the "Showing X of Y" label (it would repaint at the timer's 20 Hz for no visible change). Fix (e): **positive-only** path memo (TTL 300 s, cap 8,000) so repeat searches stop re-`stat`-ing the same folders — negatives are deliberately NEVER cached, because caching a positive only lets a deleted study linger for one TTL whereas caching a negative would make a freshly imported study refuse to appear. Deliberately not done: the streamer's batch size and 50 ms cadence are unchanged; `_resolve_renderable_study_path`'s logic (including the self-healing `force_update_study_path` write) is unchanged; the gate helpers are read via `getattr` because two separate test harnesses drive this streamer from partial stubs and a missing gate must degrade to the old behaviour rather than raise. Kill switches `AIPACS_LIST_STREAM_BACKPRESSURE=0`, `AIPACS_LIST_STREAM_BUDGET_MS=0`, `AIPACS_LIST_PATH_MEMO=0`. Report: `docs/plans/architecture/IMPORT_FREEZE_AND_YBR_COLOR_2026-08-21.md`. | `tests/code/ui_services/test_list_stream_backpressure.py` (17 guards; `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_backpressure_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_21.py` swaps in `git show HEAD:` copies and confirms **15 FAIL at HEAD**, restoring the tree in a `finally`. Load-bearing: `test_unresolved_rows_are_not_rendered_on_the_gui_thread` — the bug itself; `test_stream_resumes_where_it_stopped_when_the_worker_catches_up` — proves back-pressure loses no rows and preserves order, the one way this fix could silently drop studies; `test_a_permanently_unresolved_stream_still_makes_progress` — the escape hatch, without which a dead future hangs the list forever; `test_missing_studies_are_never_memoised` — pins the positive-only asymmetry) |

| 2026-08-21 | modules/viewer/fast (dicom_color + decode_service + lightweight_2d_pipeline) | Owner-reported: an imported ALPINION E-CUBE i7 breast ultrasound (SHUSHLEBIN DMITRY, PatientID 13996506, 45 instances, 768x1024) rendered as **coloured static**, while Myrian and other viewers showed it correctly. TWO STACKED DEFECTS, and this is the point of the row: fixing the obvious one alone changes NOTHING visible. (1) 39 of the 45 instances declare `PhotometricInterpretation` **YBR_FULL_422** but ship FULL, non-subsampled pixel data — `len(PixelData) == 2,359,296 == Rows*Cols*3`, not `*2`. pydicom 2.4.5 `numpy_handler.get_pixeldata()` computes `expected_len = R*C*3//3*2`, warns *"is a third larger than expected… you may need to change the Photometric Interpretation"*, and then **carries on anyway**: it truncates the buffer to two thirds (`pixel_data[:expected_len]`) and applies the 4:2:2 -> 4:4:4 resample with stride 4 over a period-3 interleave, so every output sample becomes a rotating mix of Y/Cb/Cr from DIFFERENT pixels. (2) Independently, the FAST pipeline painted `samples_per_pixel >= 3` frames straight into `QImage.Format_RGB888` with no YBR conversion — `dicom_color.decode_color_for_display`, which knows how, was gated behind `samples_per_pixel < 3` in `_ensure_extras`, so it never ran for a true colour image. MEASURED with row-to-row correlation (a real frame correlates between adjacent rows; scrambled samples do not), instance 22: **what AI-PACS painted = -0.355 (noise)**; adding ONLY the YBR->RGB conversion = **-0.376, still noise**; skipping only the bogus 422 expansion = 0.930 but with a heavy cyan cast (Cb/Cr ~128 landing in G/B); **both fixes = 0.931 and correct**. Fix: two new Qt-free helpers in `dicom_color.py`. `normalize_ybr_subsampling(ds)` must run **BEFORE** `ds.pixel_array` — pydicom caches the mis-decoded array, so this cannot be undone afterwards — and rewrites the tag to `YBR_FULL` only when ALL of: colour enabled, transfer syntax **uncompressed** (for encapsulated data the codec owns subsampling, not the tag), photometric exactly `YBR_FULL_422`, `SamplesPerPixel == 3`, `BitsAllocated == 8`, and `len(PixelData) >= R*C*3*frames` — so a genuinely subsampled 4:2:2 image, where pydicom's expansion is CORRECT, fails the last test and is untouched. `ybr_samples_to_rgb(ds, arr)` runs AFTER the decode and converts only if the tag STILL says YBR, because pydicom rewrites it to `RGB` when a Pillow/GDCM JPEG handler already converted — reading it post-decode is what stops a compressed colour image being converted twice. Wired into both decode paths (`decode_service._decode_worker` subprocess pool and `lightweight_2d_pipeline._decode_slice` in-process). VERIFIED through the real decoder over all 45 instances: **5/45 readable before -> 45/45 after** (median correlation -0.326 -> 0.934). REGRESSION SWEEP: one instance from each of **220 local series** decoded twice and compared byte-for-byte — **219 identical, 1 changed**, and that one belongs to the failing study; no other study in the 2,333-study database is affected. Deliberately not done: the disk pixel cache is untouched (it already refuses non-2D arrays on both `put` and read, so no colour frame is cached and none can be poisoned); `modules/printing/render/dicom_renderer.py`, `modules/cd_burner/portable_viewer/render.py` and `modules/viewer/backends/pydicom_2d_backend.py` have their own `pixel_array` calls and were NOT audited; the on-disk thumbnail for this study was generated pre-fix and stays scrambled until rebuilt. Kill switches `AIPACS_DICOM_YBR422_FIX=0`, `AIPACS_DICOM_YBR_TO_RGB=0`, master `AIPACS_DICOM_COLOR=0`. Report: `docs/plans/architecture/IMPORT_FREEZE_AND_YBR_COLOR_2026-08-21.md`. | `tests/code/viewer/test_ybr_color_decode.py` (23 guards; `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_ybr_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_21.py` swaps in `git show HEAD:` copies and confirms **19 FAIL at HEAD**, restoring the tree in a `finally`. Load-bearing: `test_decode_worker_returns_the_true_rgb_image` — end-to-end through the real decoder against an independently computed reference, the only guard that would have caught "both defects, one fixed"; `test_genuinely_subsampled_422_is_left_alone` and `test_compressed_transfer_syntax_is_left_alone` — the two ways this fix could corrupt data that works today; `test_normalisation_runs_before_the_pixel_data_is_touched` — pins the ORDERING, which is the whole mechanism; `test_conversion_is_skipped_when_a_handler_already_converted` — stops a double conversion on JPEG colour) |

| 2026-08-22 | PacsClient/pacs/workstation_ui/home_ui/patient_table_widget + PacsClient/pacs/workstation_ui/settings_ui/storage_cleanup_panel + PacsClient/pacs/patient_tab/utils/utils | Owner-reported the day after the 08-21 list fix: "there is some lag and freeze and one time the app is closed". FIRST, THE VERIFICATION THAT MATTERS: the 08-21 fix DID land — counting sampled `[MAIN_THREAD_STALL_TRACE]` stacks per day, `_resolve_renderable_study_path` went **104 -> 0** and `_progressive_render_next` **123 -> 0** (`tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_list_fix_live_2026_08_22.py`). What remained were THREE DIFFERENT GUI-thread disk scanners. **THE "CLOSE" WAS NOT A CRASH**: pid 497328 shut down cleanly at 14:17:56 (`[SHUTDOWN-INITIATOR]` from `mainwindow_ui.closeEvent` -> `QApplication.quit()` -> instance-lock release -> gateway stop, all logged) — but it closed in the **last four seconds of a 3.5-minute stall storm** (14:14:30–14:18:00, peak 13.1 s). The app was frozen and was closed. A separate `access violation` at 14:42:42 in `thumbnail_manager.CircularProgressborder.__init__` is a UNIQUE signature in a 187-record `native_fault.log`, and pid 522184 SURVIVED it and ran for another hour — a first-chance exception, not the close, and deliberately left unfixed (one occurrence, no reproduction). The other 143 fault records are `code 0x8001010d` (RPC_E_CANTCALLOUT_ININPUTSYNCCALL), which the app already guards against in `right_panel_widget` and survives. **PATH A (45+43+46 samples)** — `_refresh_statuses_chunked -> update_study_download_status -> _check_study_download_status -> check_study_complete -> sync_manifest.evaluate_sync -> build_local_manifest`. It was ALREADY chunked at 2 studies per `singleShot(0)`, with a source comment stating that only the SCHEDULING changed and everything stayed on the main thread "no worker threads and no cache races". Chunking was not enough: a 13.1 s gap spans ONE chunk, i.e. **~6.5 s per study**, because the manifest counts every `.dcm` of every series folder. Fix: new `downloadStatusReady` signal + a dedicated 2-worker pool, mirroring the `statusFlagsReady` machinery already in that file; `_compute_study_download_status` (no Qt, no widget, writes nothing) runs on the worker, `_peek_download_status` never computes, the chunk loop now only reads a cache or fires a dispatch, and `_on_download_status_ready` applies the answer on the GUI thread — dropping results from a superseded refresh (token) or a rebuilding table. The "no cache races" property is preserved BY CONSTRUCTION: worker computes, GUI thread owns every write. ALSO: `refresh_download_statuses_local_only` was a fully SYNCHRONOUS per-row loop that fires right after a storage clear, when every row is a guaranteed cache miss — the worst possible moment; it now uses the same chain. **PATH B (22 samples)** — the full sampled stack is unambiguous: `_add_socket_patient_to_table:1452 -> add_data2patient_list_table:1553 -> get_study_download_status:1618 -> count_subfolders_with_dicom:925 -> Path.rglob('*')`. That function answered "does this series folder hold any .dcm?" with `any(p.is_file() and … for p in sub.rglob('*'))` — `rglob` materialises the walk and adds a `stat` per yielded entry, so a folder with NO DICOM (thumbnails, partial download) paid a full recursive enumeration, once per row, while the table was built from a server search. MEASURED cold over the local study tree: **682.50 ms per study** vs **1.45 ms** for an early-exit `os.scandir` walk that checks a directory's FILES before descending — **470x, with 0 verdict mismatches** on every local study (`bench_count_subfolders_2026_08_22.py`). Warm it is still 2.4x. **PATH C (181 samples, 114 of them inside `shutil._rmtree_unsafe`)** — one "Clear patients" click froze the app for **183 seconds** at 10:04; `_execute_patient_cleanup` called `cleanup_patients_folder` (rmtree over the whole patients tree + the DB delete) inline. Fix: new `_CleanupWorker(QObject)`, a sibling of the `_FolderUsageWorker` already in that file for the identical reason, run on a `QThread` behind a modal indeterminate `QProgressDialog` with NO cancel button (rmtree cannot be safely interrupted mid-tree); both entry points converted — the filtered patient dialog and `_handle_cleanup_action`'s five per-category rows, which had the same inline pattern. Safe on a worker because the manager methods are pure filesystem + DB with no Qt, and `database/_pool.py` keeps connections in `threading.local`. A second cleanup while one runs is refused, not queued. Deliberately not done: `list_subfolders_with_dicom` (directly below the fixed function) keeps the `rglob` pattern — it appeared in NO sampled stall and changing it would be speculation; `_disk_series`/`_count_disk_instances` still count every instance, now merely off the GUI thread rather than cheaper. Kill switches `AIPACS_STATUS_REFRESH_OFFTHREAD=0`, `AIPACS_STATUS_REFRESH_DISPATCH`, `AIPACS_DICOM_SCAN_FAST=0`, `AIPACS_STORAGE_CLEANUP_OFFTHREAD=0`. Report: `docs/plans/architecture/GUI_THREAD_DISK_PATHS_2026-08-22.md`. | `tests/code/ui_services/test_gui_thread_disk_paths.py` (27 guards; `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_gui_disk_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_22.py` swaps in `git show HEAD:` copies and confirms **19 FAIL at HEAD**, restoring the tree in a `finally`. Load-bearing: `test_the_fast_scan_does_not_use_rglob` — monkeypatches `Path.rglob` to RAISE, so a "cleanup" that reintroduces it is caught; `test_fast_and_legacy_scans_agree` and `test_nested_dicom_still_counts` — the two ways a 470x speedup could have been bought with a wrong answer; `test_a_cold_row_is_dispatched_not_walked_on_the_gui_thread` — asserts the blocking call is NOT made, which is the whole fix; `test_a_late_answer_from_a_superseded_refresh_is_dropped` — without the token check a stale worker result repaints a row of a table that has moved on; `test_cleanup_worker_reports_failure_instead_of_raising` — an unhandled exception on a QThread kills the run silently) |

| 2026-08-23 | PacsClient/utils/native_fault_log + PacsClient/.../patient_widget_core/_pw_lifecycle + aipacs_runtime | Owner-reported after the 3.6.2 update: "the application still crashed once and closed unexpectedly". The end-user logs contain **TWO** post-update endings seven hours apart and only one is ours: 06:00:27 was an unexpected **host power loss** (`EventLog 6008` "previous system shutdown at 5:45:00 AM was unexpected", `Kernel-Power 41` with **BugcheckCode=0**, no sleep events, 304 minutes of System-log silence) — not a software defect. The real one is 00:47:02: Windows `Application Hang 1002`, `AIPacs.exe 3.6.2.0`, pid `0x6078` = **24696**, type 41, immediately after a patient-tab close. **THE FINDING IS NOT THE HANG, IT IS THAT NOTHING WE OWN COULD SEE IT.** The app's logs stop 17 s earlier at 00:46:45 (last activity: `BRIDGE_CLEANUP` ×2, `[ZetaBoost] INACTIVE`, `CLEAR_TAB`, `[SEED_CONFIG]` ×8, `[ino-approval]`) and BOTH stall diagnostics reported nothing, for two DIFFERENT structural reasons. **F8 `[MAIN_THREAD_STALL]` is a `QTimer` on the main thread** — it computes `now - last_fire_ms` when it NEXT fires, so it can only report a stall that ENDED; a block running until the process is killed produces no record, which is why pid 24696's worst recorded stall is **1 188 ms in a session that hung for 17 seconds**. **F11 `[MAIN_THREAD_STALL_TRACE]` is the one that should have caught it** — a daemon thread sampling `sys._current_frames()` once the gap passes 400 ms, no need for the block to end — but it is a **Python** thread and needs the GIL for a single bytecode; inside a long C call (`gc.collect()` over a multi-GB heap, a VTK render-window destructor, a driver call) there is no bytecode boundary, so the GIL is never released and F11 never runs. The silence of both probes IS the evidence: the block was native and held the GIL. Reading the close path, exactly ONE step is unlogged, unbounded and GIL-holding — the deferred `gc.collect()`. `BRIDGE_CLEANUP` is logged at the START of `cleanup()` (proves entry, not completion); `CLEAR_TAB tab=… removed=N` is logged at the END, so it demonstrably completed; the eight `[SEED_CONFIG]` each completed. **AND THE UNCOMFORTABLE PART: the 2026-06-27 patient-close freeze fix MOVED this freeze, it never shortened it** — its own comment records "up to ~3.7 s freeze on EVERY patient close" and the remedy was to run *the same collect* 150 ms later via `QTimer.singleShot` so the close RETURNS instantly; the collect stays on the GUI thread by design (VTK render windows destroy only there), and 150 ms after a close the user is still in front of the screen. pid 24696's heap was **2 378 MB** — the fastest ramp in the dataset (38 minutes). The three `vtkCommonCore-9.6.1.dll c0000005` crashes at offset `0x1de9770` (08-03, 08-08, 08-11) are the same teardown family: a crash and a deadlock are the two failure modes of the same unsafe VTK destruction, and a GC is what triggers it. Fix (a): `hang_watchdog()` in `native_fault_log.py` — `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later()` is implemented in C and runs its timer on its own **NATIVE** thread, so it fires *while the GIL is held* and dumps every thread's stack into the existing `native_fault.log` handle. `exit=False` (observes, never aborts); **non-reentrant on purpose** because faulthandler keeps exactly ONE timer and a nested arm would silently cancel the outer one and lose the dump; no-op without a fault-log handle; **writes nothing unless it fires**, so routine closes neither pollute the log nor confuse the block parser in `tools/diagnostics/filter_native_fault.py`; never raises. Fix (b): `_close_step()` in `_pw_lifecycle.py` — breadcrumb **BEFORE** each step and elapsed-ms after (WARNING above the threshold), watchdog armed around it. The `start` line is the load-bearing half: a step the process dies inside is now identifiable by having a start and no done. This module logs NOTHING at INFO today — every `print()` in it is redirected to `logger.debug` at the top of the file — so we could not even tell whether `exit_patient_widget` returned; it is now a thin wrapper over an unchanged `_exit_patient_widget_impl`. Fix (c): `seed_user_config_defaults` memoised per resolved `(src, dst)` — five `_config_root()` helpers (`server_profiles`, `offline_cloud`, `Identity/config`, `cloud_consultation.feature_flags`, `aipacs_chat.feature_flags`) call it on EVERY call plus three import-time sites, so merely READING a feature flag re-scanned the roaming config dir (`iterdir()` + a `stat()` per file). Keyed, not a bare bool, so tests seeding into different tmp dirs still exercise a real pass; recorded only after a COMPLETE pass so a run that bailed on a missing bundled root is retried rather than memoised away. DELIBERATELY NOT DONE, and the review recommendation was revised because of it: **`ZetaBoostDisk.clear_tab` stays on the GUI thread** — its log line is emitted last so it completed during the incident, moving it off-thread introduces a real race (a tab reopened under the same key could have its fresh entries deleted by a late clear), and it has a plugin-payload mirror that would have to move with it; the GC itself is untouched (making it generational or budgeted is a behavioural change to a path that exists to destroy VTK render windows on the GUI thread, and deserves the measurement first); bridge-teardown ordering unchanged. Kill switches `AIPACS_HANG_WATCHDOG=0`, `AIPACS_HANG_WATCHDOG_SECONDS`, `AIPACS_CLOSE_PATH_TIMING=0`, `AIPACS_CLOSE_PATH_WARN_MS`, `AIPACS_SEED_CONFIG_ONCE=0`. Report: `docs/plans/architecture/CLOSE_PATH_HANG_VISIBILITY_2026-08-23.md` + `docs/reports/ENDUSER_SANAM_STABILITY_REVIEW_2026-08-23.md`. | `tests/code/system/test_close_path_hang_visibility.py` (14) + `tests/code/runtime/test_seed_config_once.py` (12); **all 26 FAIL pre-fix**, proved by `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_close_path_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_23.py`. That script deliberately does **NOT** use `git show HEAD:` — this tree carries 389 lines of unrelated uncommitted work in `aipacs_runtime.py` and 17 in `_pw_lifecycle.py`, so restoring from HEAD would revert far more than A0 and the guards would "fail" for the wrong reason; it removes exactly the A0 additions, anchor-based, asserting every anchor before writing and restoring in a `finally`. Regression delta measured separately (`check_a0_regression_delta_2026_08_23.py`): `tests/code/{system,runtime,utils,builder}` = **580 passed, 11 failed**, and the SAME 11 fail with the A0 additions removed — **caused by A0: 0**. Load-bearing: `test_watchdog_fires_on_an_overrunning_block` — **behavioural**, a 1.2 s block under a 0.2 s watchdog must leave a real stack dump on disk, the only guard that reproduces the actual failure mode; `test_watchdog_is_silent_when_the_block_completes` — a watchdog that dumps on every close is one people turn off; `test_watchdog_is_not_reentrant` — faulthandler's single-timer trap; `test_watchdog_uses_the_gil_independent_api` — a Python thread pretending to be a watchdog passes every other test and then fails in the field exactly as F11 did; `test_close_step_logs_before_the_body_runs` — pins the ORDER, which is the whole mechanism; `test_close_path_never_depends_on_the_watchdog_importing` — a patient close must not fail because a diagnostic could not import; `test_a_failed_pass_is_not_memoised` — a missing bundled root is an install fault, not a settled answer |

VERIFIED IN SOURCE, not inferred: `_set_oblique_camera` (`_mpr_oblique.py:281-297`) runs `mapper.SliceFacesCameraOff()` + `SliceAtFocalPointOff()` and sets an explicit `vtkPlane` with `origin = self.current_position` and `normal = oblique_normal`, then comments *"Camera stays UNTOUCHED — no viewport shift."* **So the displayed oblique plane passes through the crosshair BY CONSTRUCTION** (`plane.origin IS current_position`) — there was never a geometry defect to fix. THE 1 147 FAILURES ARE A STALE VALIDATOR: `validate_after_oblique` builds its snapshots from `renderer.GetActiveCamera()` and measures the CAMERA's plane; the module header reads `Version: 2026-02-17`, i.e. it encodes the pre-Fix-E design and was never updated. Each count is explained without anything being wrong — `focal_at_crosshair` (1 046) is the in-plane offset Fix-E deliberately preserves; `plane_containment` (28) fires only on rotate-only frames, because `_apply_interaction_update` runs `["crosshairs","oblique"]` for `kind='rotate'` and SKIPS `_update_slice_positions`, leaving the camera focal's look-axis component one frame stale (the mapper's plane is still exact); `parallel_scale` (101 × 64.36 %) is the user's own zoom against a baseline captured only at view creation and after `_reset_all_to_orthogonal`. And "0 passes" is expected: violations log at WARNING always, passes only under `ZETA_MPR_DIAG=1`. **THE ROOT CAUSE OF THE NEAR-MISS WAS DOCUMENTATION.** Fix-E existed ONLY in a source docstring — no report, no catalog row, no test — while three separate documents actively described the reverted behaviour as current: `ZETA_MPR_PIPELINE_REFERENCE.md` §3.3 said `_set_oblique_camera()` *"repositions the target view's camera along the oblique normal"*; `_update_slice_positions`' own docstring said it *"updates the focal point to fully match current_position … `_synchronize_oblique_views()` will recompute it correctly"*, contradicting the code directly beneath it; and `mpr-geometry-pipeline.md` §10.6 said the canonicalization flag defaults OFF when `_BUILD_DEFAULT_CANONICALIZE = True` since 2026-06-11. Fixes, all documentation: (a) new `§10.9` do-not-break entry recording Fix-E and its consequences; (b) new `§10g` documenting the render/interaction throttle, which had NO documentation anywhere (5 ms `_request_render` batch; 16 ms `AIPACS_ZETA_MPR_INTERACT_MS` compute budget; `move ⊃ scroll ⊃ rotate` coalescing with the exact per-kind call table; 12 ms `[ZETA_MPR_PERF]` threshold) — its absence is why "just add a camera update per crosshair move" had no budget to check itself against; (c) new `§10h` documenting all nine validator checks, their thresholds, the violations-only logging asymmetry, and which three are stale; (d) a status banner mapping which sections of the governing doc are CURRENT vs HISTORICAL (§3–§6 were retired by §10b and are still written in the present tense); (e) §10.6 corrected; (f) `§10.10` recording the fixed-character-window test landmine; (g) `ZETA_MPR_PIPELINE_REFERENCE.md` §3.2/§3.3 corrected; (h) `_update_slice_positions` docstring rewritten to match its body.
