| 2026-08-23 | modules/mpr/zeta_mpr/mpr_viewer (_mpr_crosshair_interact, _mpr_crosshair_render, _mpr_layout, _mpr_oblique, _mpr_orientation, _interactor_styles) + PacsClient/.../patient_toolbar/toolbar_manager | Owner, after confirming the MPR **geometry is correct** ("even when I rotate the planes, I do not see obvious geometric distortion"): the instability is interaction. Five items, each traced to a mechanism. **(1) CROSSHAIR JITTER — the pointer was genuinely being drawn twice per frame.** The hover path drove the cursor through BOTH APIs with CONTRADICTORY values on the same frame: `_set_view_cursor(view, None)` (Qt, synchronous → arrow) and `SetCurrentCursor(10)` (VTK → crosshair), and the VTK write lands a full event-loop turn later because `QVTKRenderWindowInteractor` applies it via `QTimer.singleShot(0, ShowCursor)`. At the ~60 Hz hover cadence that is a 30–60 Hz alternation between two glyphs whose hotspots differ by 8–16 px. Aggravated by ZERO hysteresis at a boundary that is geometrically guaranteed to overlap — the 20 px centre disc lies ENTIRELY inside the 15 px line bands (`20/√2 = 14.1 < 15`), so `centre` and `h_line` share a hard edge at exactly r=20, and display coords are integers so hand tremor is a 1 px flip — and by no "only on change" guard (the computed zone was discarded by the caller, so the cursor was re-issued ~62×/s). Fix: ONE API (Qt), one write per actual change, dead-band (enter centre 20 / leave 26; enter line 15 / leave 19), cache cleared on release. **LATENT BUG FOUND WHILE READING IT:** the three drag branches in `on_mouse_move` tested `dragging_handle`/`dragging_line`/`dragging_center` WITHOUT checking `left_button_down` (the stack branch below them did) — and `dragging_center` lives on the PARENT, shared by all three panes, so one missed release (style swapped mid-gesture, toolbar tool taking over, focus lost) would let a buttonless hover keep re-centring the crosshair on the pointer, i.e. the crosshair chasing the mouse. All three now require the button. **(2) LOADING INDICATOR — there was never one dialog.** The MPR button creates FOUR independently thresholded, independently parented `QProgressDialog`s plus an in-cell placeholder: A "Loading MPR volume…" (≥80 FILES), B "Preparing MPR views…" (≥200 SLICES), C "Preparing MPR volume…" (≥200 slices), D "Building 3D volume rendering…" (NO threshold, and parented to the MPR WIDGET not the patient widget). That explains every reported symptom without the build being wrong: *open/close* = each helper deleteLater()s its own before the next exists, with an indicator-free, event-loop-free gap over `canonicalize_volume`; *reappear* = D returns in a DIFFERENT SCREEN POSITION with a different title AFTER MPR is already visible; *speed change* = an indeterminate marquee needs the event loop, and the instant C closes the `StandardMPRViewer(...)` ctor runs ~10 s with no `processEvents`, so B's marquee stops dead then jumps; *restart* = every hand-off constructs a NEW dialog whose marquee starts at phase 0, and C is created ON TOP of a visible B. Fix: `_prepare_mpr_flip_offthread` takes `existing_dlg` and reuses the caller's live modal (text only, never closes it), so C no longer stacks on B. Deliberately NOT collapsing A/B/D into one hoisted indicator yet — D's existence and default-ON flag are pinned by `test_mpr_deferred_3d_layout_stability.py::test_busy_dialog_has_a_default_on_kill_switch`, B's gate/processEvents/close by `test_mpr_open_latency_opt48.py`; those are deliberate guards and changing them is a separate explicit decision. **(3) LATENCY, incl. the VRT first-rotation delay.** The throttle was NOT the cause — it is a correct leading-edge design (first event of an idle gesture runs immediately). The real cause: `_set_active_view` ran TWICE per press and per wheel notch (Qt event filter first, then the interactor style), and each call restyled all FOUR containers — and `setStyleSheet` on a container re-polishes its whole subtree including the `QVTKRenderWindowInteractor` child, whose `paintEvent` is a full `Render()`. On the 3D pane that is a ray-cast queued BETWEEN the press and the first rotate frame. Fixes: no-op guard on re-setting the active view; restyle only the two containers that changed; **VRT press arms rotation BEFORE restyling** (`super().OnLeftButtonDown()` is what calls `StartRotate()`, which switches the window from StillUpdateRate to DesiredUpdateRate — restyling first made the queued repaint run at STILL quality); the oblique diagnostic validator no longer runs per frame (camera snapshots + numpy + ten checks + a log record, synchronously in the rotation loop — 1 152 lines in one field session — and three of its checks are stale, measuring the CAMERA's plane which has not selected the slice since v1.09.Fix-E); and `_finalize_interaction_update` clears the throttle clock so a re-press inside 16 ms is not deferred. REJECTED as invariant violations: batching the scrolled pane's own render (its immediacy is deliberate and batching would ADD latency), any `ResetCamera` on an interaction path, changing `stable_scroll_camera_step`, adding a step to the per-kind interaction call lists. **(4)+(5) TOOLBAR TOOLS CLOSED MPR — one line.** `check_and_deactivate_tools` ended its MPR branch with an unconditional `self.toggle_zeta_mpr()`, firing when `tool_selected == 'mpr'` (the state right after MPR opens) — so the FIRST tool click after opening MPR tore it down. Eight tools reached it. **On Arrow, be exact: Arrow does NOT close MPR** — it has a correct MPR branch and `activate_arrow()` is implemented; what it lacked is `handle_buttons_checked()` (Ruler and Angle both call it), so the button stayed unchecked after a successful activation, the user clicked again, and the second click took the DEACTIVATE branch — indistinguishable from "the tool broke". Fixes: the implicit close is now opt-in (`close_mpr=` param, default preserve); Curve MPR, Dental Curve MPR and the post-series-switch reset pass `close_mpr=True` explicitly (without it two MPR pipelines would fight over one grid cell — the duplicate-pipeline class the re-entrancy guard exists to prevent); **Text wired to `activate_caption()`**, which had existed in the MPR measurement tools since the refactor with NO caller, so picking Text closed MPR instead; and tools with no MPR implementation (ROI, Circle ROI, AI Chat, Two-Line Angle) now say so rather than silently running on the hidden 2D widget underneath — preserving MPR while the tool no-ops would be WORSE than the original bug. NO GEOMETRY CHANGE of any kind. Kill switches `AIPACS_MPR_HOVER_STABLE=0`, `AIPACS_MPR_ACTIVE_VIEW_NOOP_GUARD=0`, `AIPACS_MPR_PRESERVE_ON_TOOL_SELECT=0`, `AIPACS_MPR_OBLIQUE_VALIDATE=1`. Report: `docs/reports/MPR_INTERACTION_STABILITY_2026-08-23.md`. | `tests/code/mpr/test_mpr_interaction_stability.py` (20 guards). Regression surface: **2 939 passed**, 4 failed — the same four `test_local_search_progressive` pins carried since 2026-08-21; baseline before this work was 2 919 with the same four, so **0 regressions**. Load-bearing: `test_hover_never_uses_the_deferred_vtk_cursor_api` — the jitter bug itself, and it strips comments first so the prose quoting the old API cannot satisfy it; `test_hover_zones_have_hysteresis` — the overlap is geometric, so without a dead-band the boundary is guaranteed to flip; `test_every_drag_branch_requires_the_button` — the shared-parent `dragging_center` latent bug; `test_vrt_press_arms_rotation_before_restyling` — pins the ORDER, which is the whole fix; `test_the_callers_that_need_the_cell_still_close_mpr` — the one way preserving MPR could break advanced-surface switching; `test_tools_without_an_mpr_implementation_say_so` — stops the fix turning a visible bug into a silent one. ALSO: `test_mpr_flip_offthread_opt48.py::test_toolbar_passes_preflipped_to_the_viewer` was RE-PINNED, not worked around — it required the literal `self._prepare_mpr_flip_offthread(vtk_image_data)` and broke when the call gained `existing_dlg=` and wrapped onto two lines; the assertion was still true, only the spelling had moved, so it now pins the wiring rather than one line's formatting. |

| 2026-08-23 | main.py (CPU BUDGET startup block) | Owner-reported: “check the stacking in 55387; there are some lag — check if it's because of pc resource or the app” (55387 is the PATIENT id; the session that read it is pid 90364, 14:28:45–15:39:45). **THE ANSWER IS BOTH, AND ONE HALF IS OUR BUG.** First, the app's stacking work was EXONERATED by its own instrumentation over 15,759 frames: `frame_total_ms` median **1.6 ms** / p90 4.9; `disk_wait_ms`, `decode_wait_ms`, `cache_lookup_ms` and `foreground_disk_reads` are all **0.0 at both median and p90** (the pixel/frame caches are serving). Yet across 98 drag sessions `ui_lag_max_ms` median is **300.5 ms** (p90 642.5, max 1,160). **The decisive pair is `event_p95_ms` 84.5 ms against `handler_p95_ms` 9.0 ms** — a ~75 ms gap between Qt receiving the input and our handler being allowed to start; once started, our handler and our paint (`paint_max_ms` median 2.8 ms) are fast. Confirmed independently by the stall probe: 1,002 records (gap_ms median 163, p90 422, max 2,846) of which 66 carry a stack, and **45 of those 66 bottom out in `run_forever` with NOTHING below it** — the main thread was inside the Qt event loop, not executing our Python, which is the signature of WAITING, not computing. Of the 21 samples that did catch app work, **not one is on the stacking path** (5 MPR open, 5 `soundfile.write` voice save, 2 startup `setupUi`, 1 app close, 3 `stat`). And the stalls are not on the disk: 612 seconds had foreground disk, 668 had a stall, only 215 had both, and **nine of the ten busiest disk seconds recorded zero stalls**. **THE DEFECT: the app has been asking Windows for a priority boost it never received.** `GetCurrentProcess()` returns the pseudo-handle `(HANDLE)-1` == `0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF`; under ctypes' **default** `restype` of `c_int` that truncates to a 32-bit `-1`, and passing it on as an untyped argument makes the x64 call hand a 32-bit value to an API that wants a 64-bit `HANDLE`. Measured directly on this machine: `default restype -> handle=-1 -> SetPriorityClass=0, err=6` vs `c_void_p restype -> handle=0xffffffffffffffff -> SetPriorityClass=1, err=0`. **err 6 is ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE.** So `ABOVE_NORMAL_PRIORITY_CLASS` was never applied and the viewer competed for CPU on equal terms with everything else on a box that also runs a QEMU VM (**42,470 cumulative CPU-s**), Android Studio, Apache httpd, VS Code, Chrome, Proxifier, Grammarly, a VPN client and several Claude processes. **The app logged the failure on every single launch and nobody read the line** — retained logs contain **19 launches, 19 failures, 0 successes** (`app.log` + `.1/.2/.3`), including pid 90364 at 14:28:45, the very session being investigated. Fix: three ctypes declarations — `GetCurrentProcess.restype = c_void_p`, `SetPriorityClass.argtypes = [c_void_p, c_uint]`, `SetPriorityClass.restype = c_int` — placed **BEFORE** the handle is taken, because a `restype` set after the call is a no-op; two guards pin that ORDERING rather than mere presence. **VERIFIED IN THE FIELD, not only in a test**: the first launch after the fix (pid 47996, 15:40:55) logged `[CPU_BUDGET] Main process priority set to above_normal (class=0x8000)` at INFO instead of the WARNING, and the live process reports `PriorityClass = AboveNormal` — the first success ever recorded in these logs. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** no change to the stacking path (it is measurably fast — optimising it would be optimising the part that already works); no change to the throttle, cache or prefetch (nothing in the data implicates them); logging was RULED OUT as a cause, not assumed — `diagnostic_logging.py` routes every file handler behind a `QueueHandler`/`QueueListener` so log writes never run on the GUI thread; the 5 `soundfile.write` stall samples are real GUI-thread blocking but sit on the voice-note path, not the stacking path, and are left for their own investigation. **HONEST LIMITS:** `ui_lag_max_ms` is a per-drag MAXIMUM — across all 15,759 frames `ui_lag_ms` has a median of 8.9 ms, so this is one ~300 ms hitch per drag, not constant lag; the fix is proven to APPLY but its effect on `ui_lag_max_ms` is **expected, not yet demonstrated** against a post-fix reading session; and priority is a tie-breaker, not extra capacity — closing the QEMU VM remains the largest single lever and is not optional. Kill switch `AIPACS_PRIORITY=normal` (also `=high`, still not the default — HIGH can starve disk I/O and slow downloads). Report: `docs/reports/STACKING_LAG_55387_2026-08-23.md`. | `tests/code/system/test_cpu_budget_priority_boost.py` (13 guards; `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_cpu_budget_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_23.py` confirms **5 FAIL pre-fix**, restoring `main.py` in a `finally`. That script CAN use `git show HEAD:main.py` — and asserts it may: `git diff --numstat -- main.py` is exactly `18  0  main.py`, the fix hunk and nothing else, so unlike the A0 script there is no unrelated uncommitted work to revert; it refuses to run if that stops being true. Load-bearing: `test_restype_is_set_before_the_handle_is_taken` and `test_argtypes_are_set_before_setpriorityclass_is_called` — they pin the ORDER, and a “tidy-up” that moves the declarations below the call passes every presence test and silently restores the bug; `test_untyped_pseudo_handle_is_rejected_by_windows` / `test_typed_pseudo_handle_is_accepted_by_windows` — **behavioural**, reproducing the truncation against the real Win32 API through the read-only `GetPriorityClass` (same HANDLE argument, no side effect on the test runner's own priority); `test_failure_is_still_reported_not_swallowed` — the `err=6` line is the ONLY reason this was ever found, so removing the diagnostic is treated as a regression; `test_normal_escape_hatch_preserved` — the kill switch. Regression surface: `tests/code/{system,runtime,utils,builder}` = **593 passed, 11 failed**, and the 11 are byte-identical to the 08-23 A0 baseline (580 passed + the 13 new guards = 593), so **0 regressions**.) |

| 2026-08-23 | main.py (CPU BUDGET startup block) — follow-up to the row above | Owner, immediately after the priority fix landed: “fix this in a way that, on other pc it use the high priority.” **“Other PC” needs a rule the app can EVALUATE AT STARTUP**, and the discriminator chosen is **build type**, because it is exactly what separates the owner's development box from every deployed workstation and needs no per-PC setup: a frozen/installed build (`AIPacs.exe`) is a clinical workstation whose whole job is this app → **HIGH_PRIORITY_CLASS (0x00000080)**; a source run (`python main.py`) is a developer box simultaneously running an IDE, a QEMU VM, a compiler and a test suite → **ABOVE_NORMAL (0x00008000)**, because promoting the app under test above the tools being used to develop it makes the machine worse, not better. Detection uses `aipacs_runtime.is_frozen()` — the canonical helper, which understands PyInstaller (`sys.frozen`) AND Nuitka (`__compiled__` / `sys.__nuitka__`, normalised by the bootstrap at the top of `main.py`) — imported locally because `aipacs_runtime` is already imported at module scope, and wrapped so a failing probe degrades to the **source** default, never to HIGH. `AIPACS_PRIORITY = normal|above_normal|high` still overrides both and `normal` remains the kill switch. **ALSO FIXED IN THE SAME LINE:** an unrecognised `AIPACS_PRIORITY` used to fall back to a hard-coded `0x00008000`, so a typo on a clinical workstation would have silently demoted it from HIGH; the fallback is now `_pri_map[_pri_default]`. The success log gained `default=%s, frozen=%s` because on an end-user machine the log is the only way to tell what was decided. Verified on the dev box: `is_frozen()` is `False`, so **nothing changes there**. **THE HIGH CAVEAT IS REAL AND EXPOSED A SECOND DEFECT.** The block's original comment refused HIGH because “it can starve disk I/O and make downloads slower”, and the codebase's mitigation for that is that the DM download subprocess demotes ITSELF to BELOW_NORMAL. **`modules/download_manager/workers/download_process_entry.py:149` carries the IDENTICAL ctypes pseudo-handle defect — no `restype`, no `argtypes`, and it does not even check the return value, so unlike `main.py` it never logged a failure. The download subprocess has been running at Normal all along.** The consequence is larger than one missing demotion: that same file's v2.3.7 comment reasons from the premise “the viewer (ABOVE_NORMAL) blocks waiting on a lock held by an IDLE-scheduled thread in the subprocess, producing the exact `event_p95` spikes while `handler_p95` stays low pattern observed” — **that premise was false** (the viewer was at Normal), and the pattern it names is precisely what pid 90364 measured (`event_p95` 84.5 ms vs `handler_p95` 9.0 ms). The intended priority separation has never existed at runtime: both ends were Normal. **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE:** fixing it changes scheduling on the download path where this repo has MEASURED harm from widening the gap (`ui_lag_max` 412 ms vs 229 ms when the subprocess dropped to IDLE), and viewer HIGH (base 13) against downloader BELOW_NORMAL (6) is a **wider** gap than the ABOVE_NORMAL/IDLE pairing that was measured as harmful — so it needs its own decision and its own measurement, not a silent fix attached to this one. **Until then `high` is untested against heavy concurrent downloading; fall back with `AIPACS_PRIORITY=above_normal` on any workstation where downloads regress.** Report: `docs/reports/STACKING_LAG_55387_2026-08-23.md` §“Follow-up”. | `tests/code/system/test_cpu_budget_priority_boost.py` (now **23 guards**; **16 FAIL pre-fix**, proved by `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_cpu_budget_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_23.py`, which was upgraded for this landing: instead of asserting a literal `18  0  main.py` numstat it now **proves** `git show HEAD:` is safe by parsing `git diff -U0` hunk headers and requiring every hunk to fall inside the CPU BUDGET block's line range at HEAD — it refuses to run otherwise. The build-type guards are **BEHAVIOURAL, not source pins**: `_resolution_source()` lifts the resolution lines out of `main.py` by anchor (from the START of the anchor's line, so `textwrap.dedent` has a common prefix) and `exec`s them against a stubbed `_pri_frozen` and a stubbed `os.environ`, so they test what the shipped code actually decides. Load-bearing: `test_frozen_builds_default_to_high` and `test_source_runs_default_to_above_normal` — the policy itself; `test_unknown_value_falls_back_to_the_machine_default_not_a_constant` — the silent-demotion trap, which a presence-only guard cannot see; `test_a_broken_frozen_probe_degrades_to_the_safe_default` — a probe failure must never promote a machine to HIGH; `test_frozen_detection_uses_the_canonical_helper` — a bare `sys.frozen` check would report False on every Nuitka build, i.e. on the exact machines this change is for. **TWO GUARDS WERE RE-PINNED, NOT DELETED, and both say so in the file:** `test_normal_escape_hatch_preserved` (the default argument moved from the literal `'above_normal'` to `_pri_default` — same property, new spelling) and `test_high_priority_class_is_not_the_default` → `test_high_is_the_default_only_for_installed_builds` — **the POLICY changed by request**, so the guard now pins the NEW rule, which still catches a later edit that quietly makes HIGH the default for source runs too. Regression surface — MEASURED, and the scope is stated because it shrank: `tests/code/{system,runtime,utils}` = **519 passed, 1 xfailed, 4 failed**, and the four are the `test_local_search_progressive` pins carried since 2026-08-21, so **0 regressions**. **`tests/code/builder` was NOT re-run for this landing** — `test_release_parity_guards.py` calls `builder/release_gate.check_source_freshness()`, which shells out to `git fetch` with a 90 s timeout, and that fetch hung on this network. That hardened ONE call site; the cause (re-entering the event loop mid-switch) was never addressed, so the same race simply moved from the FADE to CONSTRUCTION. Fix (a) **the cause**: `show_overlay` now paints with `overlay.repaint()` — synchronous, paints immediately, does NOT run the event loop — so nothing can nest; the old double `processEvents()` survives only inside the kill-switch else-branch, and a guard asserts it can never sit on the default path again. Fix (b) **defence in depth**: `switch_series` refuses a re-entrant call on the same container and clears its flag in a `finally` that also covers the early `return False` — a stuck flag would turn a crash into a permanently dead pane. This is not redundant with (a): `processEvents()` is called from a dozen other places in this codebase and ANY of them reached during a switch could nest again. Note the existing same-series no-op could NOT have caught it — the first switch of the crash carried an **empty `series_uid`**, so the identity comparison never matched. Fix (c) `AiPacsLoadingOverlay.__init__` refuses a destroyed anchor **before** anything touches it (`_anchor_has_native_render_window`, `super().__init__(anchor)`, `_sync_geometry`, `installEventFilter` all dereference it); raising is safe because both callers of `show_overlay` already try/except and degrade to no overlay. `_widget_is_alive` falls back to **True** when `shiboken6` is unimportable — a missing probe must never be read as “widget is dead”. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** no change to what the overlay looks like or when it is shown; the `QTimer.singleShot(180, _safe_hide_spinner)` hide path is untouched; the 2026-06-05 fade guard is left exactly as it is and is pinned by a new test so this fix cannot regress it. **KNOWN DIAGNOSTIC GAP, worth closing separately:** faulthandler dumps in `native_fault.log` are **not stamped with a pid**, so this dump had to be attributed to 217556 by its stack contents and timing — the `=== session start ===` header immediately above it belongs to pid 178048, which wrote nothing to app.log at all. Kill switches `AIPACS_OVERLAY_SYNC_PAINT=0`, `AIPACS_SWITCH_REENTRANCY_GUARD=0`, `AIPACS_OVERLAY_ANCHOR_GUARD=0`. Report: `docs/reports/OVERLAY_REENTRANCY_CRASH_2026-08-26.md`. | `tests/code/system/test_overlay_reentrancy_crash.py` (13 guards; **11 FAIL pre-fix**, proved by `tools/analysis/oneoff/verify_overlay_reentrancy_guard_fails_prefix_2026_08_26.py`, which restores ONLY the two changed files — this tree carries unrelated uncommitted work in `toolbar_manager.py`, `_pw_panels.py` and the builder outputs — and refuses to run if HEAD already has the fix. Source pins are **AST-bounded**, never a character window, and `_func_src` takes a CLASS scope because `loading_overlay.py` defines three `__init__`s and a bare `ast.walk()` returns `_LogoSpinner.__init__` first — a guard bound to the wrong one guards nothing. Load-bearing: `test_a_nested_switch_is_refused` — **behavioural, reproduces the crash without Qt** by binding the REAL `switch_series` to a plain stub and calling it re-entrantly from inside `_start_qt_viewer`, exactly as `processEvents()` used to; `test_the_flag_clears_when_the_switch_raises` — the dead-pane failure mode, and it asserts a LATER switch still works; `test_anchor_guard_runs_before_anything_touches_the_anchor` — pins the ORDER, which is the whole mechanism; `test_processevents_survives_only_behind_the_kill_switch` — stops the cause being quietly restored; `test_widget_is_alive_is_conservative_without_shiboken` — a missing probe must not be read as ‘dead’; `test_the_2026_06_05_fade_liveness_guard_is_still_there` — prior art must survive. ### FAST embedded-viewer replacement window (2026-09-01)

| Date | Module | Failure and correction | Regression guard |

| 2026-09-01 | `PacsClient/.../vtk_widget/qt_fast_container.py` | During an uncached FAST drop, the intentional one-frame Preview -> Complete promotion replaces the embedded `QtSliceViewer`. Both install paths removed the old visible child from the layout, called `setParent(None)`, and only then scheduled `deleteLater()`. On Windows that detached child becomes a top-level native widget until the event loop delivers deletion, producing the reported millisecond window flash. `_retire_embedded_viewer_widget` now hides the child, retains its container parent/ownership, and defers deletion. Preview promotion, force reload, series identity, decode, and Advanced/VTK paths are unchanged. | Two guards in `tests/code/viewer/test_viewport_drop_replacement.py` failed before the fix (missing helper and two detach sites) and pass afterward. 1. **Every fix gets a row.** No exceptions. The PR that lands the fix adds the row in the same diff.
2. **Module column lists every subsystem touched.** A bug that crossed `download_manager` and `viewer` is two rows in the Module cell.
3. **Guard test must FAIL on the pre-fix codebase** and PASS on the fixed one. Without that property, the row is decorative.
4. **Cross-module bugs go under `tests/code/system/test_<date>_<slug>.py`.** Single-module bugs live next to the module under `tests/code/<module>/test_<short>_regression.py`.
5. **Don't remove rows.** If a guard becomes obsolete (the surface it protects was deleted), mark the row with `(retired YYYY-MM-DD)` and keep it for archaeology.

## Viewer Configuration storage cleanup safety and performance (2026-09-12)

| Boundary | Defect and correction | Regression guard |
|---|---|---|
| Managed storage sizing | `Path.rglob` plus per-entry `is_file`/`stat` made the full scan exceed 90 seconds. One link-safe `os.scandir` pass now supplies byte and file totals; measured full scan is 2.915 seconds. | `tests/code/storage/test_storage_cleanup_consistency.py::test_directory_size_uses_single_pass_scandir_not_path_rglob` |
| Patient deletion integrity | Filtered cleanup trusted external `study_path` values and swallowed file deletion failures before removing DB rows. Paths must resolve beneath patient storage; failed patients retain their DB index and surface warnings. Full cleanup also preserves the DB after any filesystem failure and explicitly deletes child tables without relying on FK mode. | containment, file-lock, Clear ALL failure, and cascade-independent cases in `test_storage_cleanup_filtered.py` and `test_storage_cleanup_consistency.py` |
| Retention authority | Acquisition date could delete an old exam imported recently, and unknown ages could be guessed into the oldest set. `studies.imported_at` is primary, with download/acquisition compatibility fallback; undatable rows are kept. | `test_recent_local_import_is_not_deleted_because_acquisition_is_old`; `test_delete_oldest_never_guesses_the_age_of_undatable_patients` |
| Qt worker and UI-thread boundary | Bare signal lambdas executed cleanup completion UI on the worker; panel-owned running QThreads could be destroyed; Preview, consistency, and drive probes could block the GUI. QObject slots, process-owned jobs, a shutdown boundary, and worker execution correct the ownership. | thread-affinity, parent ownership, preview/consistency, drive-probe and teardown cases in `test_storage_cleanup_panel_async.py` |
| Concurrent storage users | Cleanup could race import, download, or an open patient viewer. The app owner now injects one fail-closed activity probe and destructive cleanup is refused until the storage boundary is idle. | `test_storage_cleanup_panel_async.py::test_activity_probe_fails_closed_before_destructive_cleanup`; adjacent settings/download-state suites |

### Fresh source native entry receipt (2026-09-19)

The user explicitly requested launch with the test flag. No source main.py process
or application window remained, so one .venv source instance was launched with
AIPACS_TEST_SERVER=1. Authentication was not automated. Home became ready; fresh
ping and 77-action discovery succeeded. Native Eagle Eye navigation showed the new
header button. Clicking it opened the embedded Total Spine tab before Advanced had
loaded any pixels. Background discovery returned eligible radiographs. Native
selection/loading rendered both requested coronal and lateral source images, and
switching projections preserved the loaded coronal image. Patient identifiers and
receipts remain private. This verifies native entry, independent loading and display;
it does not certify native endplate dragging, numbering changes, inference or export
in this run. Those interaction/geometry contracts have automated/offscreen guards.
The editor remains open for reader correction. No clinical result was signed.


### Choose Function and results-first review (2026-09-19)

This supersedes the preceding direct-header-button flow. Total Spine is selected
through Choose Function alongside the existing DX/CR functions. The picker can
resolve study modalities from the local catalog on an owned background worker
before the viewer loads. Mixed-modality catalogs ask which image type to use.
The separate Total Spine header button is removed.

The preparation window requests a coronal image and a two-corner spine region;
that second corner automatically runs the local ScolioVis detector. This minimal
input is still required: projection descriptions alone are not reliable enough,
and no validated automatic spine-region detector is installed. No acquisition or
numbering confirmation is fabricated. Results include unnumbered endplates and a
physical-pixel-aspect maximum-angle proposal with classic Cobb construction.
Only successful nonempty results reveal the reusable Total Spine Alignment tab,
appended after Reception Data. Cancellation or failure keeps preparation retryable.
Lateral angles, rotation and clinical curve selection still require reader input;
the coronal model does not silently run on a lateral image.

Review controls use collapsible sections: numbering and endplate correction are
expanded, while detection, calibration and detailed assessment are collapsed.
Source image switching, report evidence gates and manual corrections are preserved.

Regression: test_total_spine_workspace.py failed three new behavior guards before
implementation (extra header button, premature tab, missing automatic transition).
It also covers canceled/empty results, off-thread catalog resolution, physical-angle
ordering and narrow-sidebar layout. Focused code suite: 131 passed, six third-party
deprecation warnings. Mirror verification: 468 pairs match, no plugin-only files.
Private offscreen preview reuses existing source-image proposals and was inspected;
it is not a new AI inference receipt or live application acceptance. The existing
source session responds to ping/list_actions but predates this revision. Fresh
source launch/sign-in and native Choose Function -> preparation -> result-tab
acceptance remain pending. No process restart or authentication was automated.
